class="hero_heroButton_31K" href="/membership">Become a CAPA Member</a></div></div><div></div><section id="main-section"><div><div class=""><div class="container"><div class="home_container_3k7"><div class="home_sidebar_2CK"><h1><a href="/analysis/reports">Latest Analysis</a></h1><a class="home_button_2r_" href="/analysis/reports">View More</a></div><div class="home_analysisItems_3zs"><div style="flex: 1 1 0%;"><div class="linkBox_analysis_cfI undefined"><div class="linkBox_header_3GR"><div class="linkBox_type_2nv">Premium Analysis</div><a class="linkBox_image_38u" href="https://centreforaviation.com/analysis/reports/ulcc-spirit-airlines-investors-wary-as-short-term-challenges-mount-494495" style='background-image: url("https://images.cdn.centreforaviation.com/stories/nkcover-1200x.jpg");'>ULCC Spirit Airlines' investors wary as short term challenges mount</a></div><div class="linkBox_article_2Ow"><div class="linkBox_title_1Js"><div class="linkBox_date_3CV">23-Oct-2019</div><h2><a href="https://centreforaviation.com/analysis/reports/ulcc-spirit-airlines-investors-wary-as-short-term-challenges-mount-494495">ULCC Spirit Airlines' investors wary as short term challenges mount</a></h2></div><div class="linkBox_abstractContainer_2aq linkBox_overflowing_1L4"><div class="linkBox_abstract_1YI" style="height: 144px;"><p>It has been a tough 2019 for Spirit Airlines, which is facing cost pressure from the recent fallout from Hurricane Dorian and from the airline's earlier decision to drive up utilisation. The company’s projected unit revenue performance for 3Q3019 is weaker than those of its US industry peers.</p>
<p>All of those factors are creating obstacles for Spirit in achieving favourable profit for the remainder of the year. </p>
<p>Those headwinds are occurring as Spirit appears to be pivoting toward becoming a more “value” airline – working to engender a more positive sentiment among customers through improved operations and small changes to its aircraft cabins to create a bit more comfort. </p>
<p>In the short term, Spirit’s valuation may continue stalling as markets look for signs of improvement beginning in 2020. The company’s preliminary cost guidance for 2020 is not producing a lot of market confidence.   </p></div></div></div><div class="clearFix"></div></div></div><div class="home_columns_1k4"><div class="home_column_3Df"><div style="flex: 1 1 0%;"><div class="linkBox_analysis_cfI linkBox_medium_13R"><div class="linkBox_header_3GR"><div class="linkBox_type_2nv">Premium Analysis</div><a class="linkBox_image_38u" href="https://centreforaviation.com/analysis/reports/air-canada-marks-a-decade-of-transformation-the-evolution-continues-495244" style='background-image: url("https://images.cdn.centreforaviation.com/stories/acpix-600x.jpg");'>Air Canada marks a decade of transformation; the evolution continues</a></div><div class="linkBox_article_2Ow"><div class="linkBox_title_1Js"><div class="linkBox_date_3CV">22-Oct-2019</div><h2><a href="https://centreforaviation.com/analysis/reports/air-canada-marks-a-decade-of-transformation-the-evolution-continues-495244">Air Canada marks a decade of transformation; the evolution continues</a></h2></div><div class="linkBox_abstractContainer_2aq"><div class="linkBox_abstract_1YI" style="height: 0px;"><p>The global aviation industry is so fast moving that there is little time to take a pause and gauge the strides that certain airlines have made in turning their businesses around.</p>
<p>A decade ago Air Canada was nearly on the verge of another bankruptcy filing, and essentially had to reinvent itself from the ground up in nearly all facets of its business – cultural, financial and customer experience. </p>
<p>A decade later, Air Canada’s reinvention is complete, and the company deserves credit for sticking to its plans despite some initial scepticism about its growth targets. While it still faces inevitable challenges that have recently included the grounding of its Boeing 737 MAX narrowbodies and other geopolitical difficulties, Air Canada should be able now to withstand those inevitable difficulties while continuing to deliver solid results.</p></div></div></div><div class="clearFix"></div></div></div><div style="flex: 1 1 0%;"><div class="linkBox_analysis_cfI linkBox_small_3ll"><div class="linkBox_header_3GR"><div class="linkBox_type_2nv">Analysis</div><a class="linkBox_image_38u" href="https://centreforaviation.com/about/press-releases/capa--aviation-week-network-airline-operations-leaders-summit-496988" style='background-image: url("https://images.cdn.centreforaviation.com/stories/Sevillecopy-300x.jpg");'>CAPA &amp; Aviation Week Network: Airline Operations Leaders' Summit</a></div><div class="linkBox_article_2Ow"><div class="linkBox_title_1Js"><div class="linkBox_date_3CV">22-Oct-2019</div><h2><a href="https://centreforaviation.com/about/press-releases/capa--aviation-week-network-airline-operations-leaders-summit-496988">CAPA &amp; Aviation Week Network: Airline Operations Leaders' Summit</a></h2></div><div class="linkBox_abstractContainer_2aq"><div class="linkBox_abstract_1YI" style="height: 0px;"><p>The inaugural <a href="https://seville19.capaevents.com/">Airline Operations Leaders' Summit</a>, organised by <a href="https://centreforaviation.com/">CAPA - Centre for Aviation </a>(CAPA) and <a href="https://aviationweek.com/">Aviation Week Network</a>, will take place on December 2-3 in Seville, Spain, at the hotel Barceló Sevilla Renacimiento. </p>
<p>It will cover aviation material costs, as well as challenges in cybersecurity, supply and labour, with aerospace, aftermarket and MRO data, and leading airline strategy, news and events.</p>
<p>Attendees of the summit will hear from the highest calibre of aviation industry and airline thought leaders.</p></div></div></div><div class="clearFix"></div></div></div></div><div class="home_column_3Df"><div style="flex: 1 1 0%;"><div class="linkBox_analysis_cfI linkBox_small_3ll"><div class="linkBox_header_3GR"><div class="linkBox_type_2nv">Analysis</div><a class="linkBox_image_38u" href="https://centreforaviation.com/analysis/reports/big-changes-and-domestic-aviation-market-reshuffles-in-peru-496409" style='background-image: url("https://images.cdn.centreforaviation.com/stories/2019/Oct/08/perupic-300x.jpg");'>Big changes and domestic aviation market reshuffles in Peru</a></div><div class="linkBox_article_2Ow"><div class="linkBox_title_1Js"><div class="linkBox_date_3CV">22-Oct-2019</div><h2><a href="https://centreforaviation.com/analysis/reports/big-changes-and-domestic-aviation-market-reshuffles-in-peru-496409">Big changes and domestic aviation market reshuffles in Peru</a></h2></div><div class="linkBox_abstractContainer_2aq"><div class="linkBox_abstract_1YI" style="height: 0px;"><p>Peru’s domestic aviation landscape has undergone significant changes in the last year and these culminated in the recent suspension of service by Peruvian Airlines, which had risen to become the country’s second largest airline.</p>
<p>But the fate of Peruvian and other airlines has increasingly been called into question as low cost operators Viva Air Peru and Sky continue to grow in the market. </p>
<p>LC Peru, which had nearly an 8% share of Peru’s domestic in 2018, has now ceased operations, and the question will be whether Peruvian can revive itself. Avianca Peru has turned its attention to international destinations, which are also creating some opportunities in the country’s domestic market. </p>
<p>Even with all the jockeying occurring in Peru’s domestic market, the long-standing market leader LATAM Airlines Peru is maintaining its dominance and is also looking at ways it can take advantage of shifts in the domestic landscape. </p></div></div></div><div class="clearFix"></div></div></div><div style="flex: 1 1 0%;"><div class="linkBox_analysis_cfI linkBox_medium_13R"><div class="linkBox_header_3GR"><div class="linkBox_type_2nv">Premium Analysis</div><a class="linkBox_image_38u" href="https://centreforaviation.com/analysis/reports/iags-net-zero-carbon-target-global-aviation-must-follow-its-lead-496809" style='background-image: url("https://images.cdn.centreforaviation.com/stories/new_image_database/storm_sky_cloud_uncertainty-600x.jpg");'>IAG's net zero carbon target. Global aviation must follow its lead</a></div><div class="linkBox_article_2Ow"><div class="linkBox_title_1Js"><div class="linkBox_date_3CV">21-Oct-2019</div><h2><a href="https://centreforaviation.com/analysis/reports/iags-net-zero-carbon-target-global-aviation-must-follow-its-lead-496809">IAG's net zero carbon target. Global aviation must follow its lead</a></h2></div><div class="linkBox_abstractContainer_2aq"><div class="linkBox_abstract_1YI" style="height: 0px;"><p>On 10-Oct-2019 IAG became the first airline group in the world to commit to net zero carbon emissions by 2050. This moves it ahead of the global airline industry's long held target to halve emissions by 2050.</p>
<p>The aviation industry's halving target has been made to look inadequate compared with the United Nations newer and more ambitious target for the wider global economy to eliminate emissions. Governments in the UK, France, Norway and Sweden have committed in law to net zero emissions, and many others are moving towards this target.</p>
<p>Aviation led on emissions reduction targets, but leading the way more than a decade ago is no longer good enough. Aviation contributes only 2%-5% of global CO2 emissions, but a flight adds hugely to an individual's carbon footprint; there is rising awareness of climate change and a growing threat of a backlash against aviation.</p>
<p>CAPA has urged aviation to align itself with the more ambitious zero emissions target in a number of reports over the past year. IAG's move is therefore very welcome, but the industry must act in unison on this crucial issue. Perhaps IAG's commitment can be a catalyst for the whole of aviation to heed CAPA's call.</p></div></div></div><div class="clearFix"></div></div></div></div></div></div><div class="clearFix"></div></div></div></div><div class="ad_ad_2n2"></div><div class="home_stripe_3DL"><div class="container"><div class="home_container_3k7"><div class="home_sidebar_2CK"><h1><a <ul>
<li>Airlines should be able to use any peak period slot for flights servicing regional New South Wales;</li>
<li>Measuring the number of actual aircraft movements once an hour would help to achieve the intended 80 movements an hour, and benefit airlines and their passengers;</li>
<li>Alternative types of freight aircraft should be allowed to operate during the curfew, provided aircraft noise and the number of movements are not increased above current levels. [<a data-mce-href="https://centreforaviation.com/members/direct-news/economic-regulation-of-airports-498303" href="https://centreforaviation.com/members/direct-news/economic-regulation-of-airports-498303">more - original PR</a>]</li>

French Bee, which have long haul expansion plans (including Paris-New York launches from both next summer).&nbsp;Corsair's growth plans have been renewed following its sale by TUI to Intro Aviation.</p>\r\n<p>At the same time Air Corsica, the biggest French airline by seats that is not part of Air France-KLM, continues to operate its domestic-focused network.</p>\r\n<p>Just as Air France looks for a path to improved profitability, competition could be intensifying.</p>","premium":1,"source":"","url":"https://centreforaviation.com/analysis/reports/air-france-klm-under-pressure-from-new-dynamics-495481","image":"https://images.cdn.centreforaviation.com/stories/new_image_database/countries/france/france_paris_aircraft_inflight.jpg","type":{"id":1,"name":"Analysis"}}]},{"type":"video","title":"CAPA
